### FR-48-50 — The breakpoint rule
For a draft threshold `t` on its width basis, and the SGS tiers `T ∈ {768, 1024}` (Spec 35 D2):
1. **Snap** when `min over T of |t − T| ≤ 0.05 × T` (5% of the tier). The nearest tier wins: 1060 snaps to 1024 (|1060 − 1024| = 36 ≤ 51.2). Every snap writes a divergence-ledger entry automatically (§3.5): rule `breakpoint-snap`, the node, the draft threshold, the tier and the distance in px, and `decided` naming this rule. It is a house-rule entry, so it cites no register item. Spec 32 FR-32-13 allows this snap (Bean's decision); the residual rule there covers only a threshold outside the tolerance. **Why 5% (provisional):** it lets a draft threshold that sits a few dozen pixels from a tier (1060 against 1024, 36px) use the tier the client already edits, while every Indus threshold that is not already a tier (1200 and 1260, 17% and 23% above 1024) stays off-tier and keeps its exact width. The `breakpoint-snap` entries from the Indus run move it: a snapped threshold whose layout between the draft threshold and the tier differs visibly from the draft (an open row in that width band, or Bean's eye) lowers it; no such row across the snaps leaves it or raises it.
2. Otherwise use the block's own breakpoint setting when one exists (for example a header's burger breakpoint). A structured setting is preferred to custom CSS wherever one exists.
3. Otherwise write the block's custom CSS (`sgsCustomCss`) with a scoped media query, under Spec 32 FR-32-13's precedence rules and FR-32-20's hardening, and list it in the surface report. Route-written CSS carries the label FR-32-20 defines, so the client can see which rules the route wrote.

**Prerequisite (met 2026-10-12).** Spec 32 FR-32-20 is built: `plugins/sgs-blocks/includes/custom-css.php::render_custom_css` parses every rule through `includes/class-custom-css-scoper.php::Custom_Css_Scoper` and confines it to the instance (rules inside `@media` included; what cannot be confined is refused), takes the scope class from the instance key `sgsCustomCssId`, lifts core blocks' CSS to the head too, scopes the editor canvas through the same rules, and shows the route's label (`sgsCustomCssNote`) in the inspector. Indus's header branches at 1200 and 1260 (`Indus Foods Website v2.dc.html`), which no tier snaps to, so the route writes them through this channel.

*Done when:* a 1060 threshold writes the 1024 tier and one `breakpoint-snap` ledger entry with distance 36, and the flip check passes at 1023/1025; a 1200 threshold writes a bounded, scoped, labelled rule that flips at 1199/1201 and leaves 375 and 1440 unchanged; and an unscoped test rule is refused.

Status: built (2026-10-22, wave T2b, `0d313aecd`, `2cf708e6a`, review `50a8c5ebf`): `scripts/computed-route/lib/fill-width-gate.mjs::widthGateWrites` reads each sc-if's width condition from the fact record and writes the hidden range: the tier flags (`sgsHideOnMobile`, `sgsHideOnTablet`, `sgsHideOnDesktop`, each checked against the block in the DB), a block that draws its own alternative below a threshold its collapse setting (`rules.navigation.collapseAttribute`, never a hiding flag), a threshold within 5% of a tier snapped with a `breakpoint-snap` entry written into the client's divergence ledger (`lib/ledger.mjs::RULES`, `lint.mjs::HOUSE_RULES`), any other threshold a scoped `@media` rule in `sgsCustomCss` labelled in `sgsCustomCssNote`. The skeleton keeps elements that render only below 1440 (`lib/skeleton-inventory.mjs::withWidthOnly`, `lib/skeleton-model.mjs` `blockKids`/`widthKids`). Live on indus-test post 201: the top bar's phone branch, the tablet CTA, the CTA hidden below 1260 (pairs `sites/indus-foods/build/qa/pairs/header-2026-10-22-t2b/`). Per-width variation (2026-10-23, the header-looks wave, Bean's ruling): content no single setting holds (a button's "Call" label below 768) is one instance per variation (`lib/skeleton-propose.mjs::widthVariant`; a words-only variant shares its default's `draftRef` and names its words element in `variantKey`, and re-decides the attributes its words decide, `wordBound`), each hidden on the other's widths (`fill-width-gate.mjs::shownOfNode`), its reads masked where it is hidden (`maskHiddenVariants`) and its content read at the widest width it is drawn at (`fill-resolve.mjs::contentWidth`) (`71b2d9da6`, `01cc6b49c`, `080a7209c`, `c0ee5273a`, `0778e71c6`); a style value that differs by width is never refused: `lib/fill-width-ladder.mjs` writes it as an equal-in-effect value, a tier-shaped setting, the block's breakpoint or a scoped `@media` rule (`870014435`, `eb7fd384b`, `7ae0ff51b`). Live on post 201: pairs `sites/indus-foods/build/qa/pairs/header-2026-10-23-looks/`. Open: Eye Care's 1060 snap checked on its rebuilt header (Stage S step 7); a step between the read widths goes through a scoped `@media` rung from the sweep (`lib/fill-step-rungs.mjs`, `96bcc851b`), but only on the measured items (the nav's 1024-1199px item padding lands on `:nth-of-type(3)` and `(7)`, never every item).

### 10.1 Recognisers and the coverage waves

One recogniser framework serves every wave that needs one: a mechanism signature, read from the re-derived inventory (FR-48-25), the census (FR-48-24) and the styles sampled in the harness's sample mode (FR-48-28), identifies a behaviour or effect and its options by what the code does, never by names in the draft, and maps it to an SGS setting by framework-DB query (FP-1: each behaviour and content setting's meaning, trigger and parameters; Indus delivery U11 for the settings Indus maps), or raises a gap that names the mechanism. Intake proposes one review row per recognised effect or behaviour and Bean confirms the mapping once; Fill writes it. Each wave below closes one group of coverage-map gaps; the delivery plans order them by their client's gap list (`.claude/plans/2026-10-21-handoff-readers.md`), and each wave's own list is the lines of `capabilities.mjs --gaps` that name it.

### FR-48-77 — Effect recognition: draft motion to SGS effects

A draft's interactive and scroll effects are usually written in its own plain JavaScript, while SGS expresses the same effects as block settings and Spec 38 effects, some of them GSAP (Tier G). FR-48-47 covers entrances, hover transitions and looping animations the inventory records, so without this step a clone silently drops every script-driven effect. This is the first recogniser of the family above.

**Read:** the re-derived inventory (FR-48-25) with its listeners and their targets, the census, and the styles that scroll and pointer handlers write per width in the harness's sample mode (FR-48-28). **Recognise** each effect by its mechanism, never by names in the draft: a section held in place over a scroll range while a scroll handler writes a translate on a wider track is a pinned horizontal scroll; a pointer handler writing custom properties that a `clip-path` or `mask` on a layer inside its target consumes is a pointer-tracked reveal; a pointer handler translating its target toward the pointer is a magnet; one writing `rotateX`/`rotateY` from the pointer is a tilt; an endless translate on a duplicated track is a marquee; a property written in proportion to scroll progress is a scrub. **Map** each recognised effect to the SGS setting that expresses it, chosen from the framework DB (the `fx_effects` rows and each block's declared effect support, for example `sgs/timeline` with its `scrollEffect` `pinned-horizontal`, which runs the `horizontal-panel` effect), never from a hand-written list, with its parameters (distance, duration, easing, radius) from the recorded values. **Write:** intake proposes one review row per recognised effect (a new Appendix C row type, `effect`) and Bean confirms the effect-to-setting choice once; Fill writes the confirmed effect as per-element `sgsMotion` values (Spec 38A §6.2, §14, FR-38A-33) on the block the skeleton chose, into whichever of the three motion panels owns it (`hover` for a magnet, tilt or sheen, `entrance` for a reveal, `effect` for a pinned track, cursor reveal, parallax or marquee) with its recorded parameters; the cursor reveal maps to the `cursor-field` `image-reveal` type (Spec 38A FR-38A-34). **Gap:** an effect that no `fx_effects` row or block setting expresses is a gap owned by Spec 38 that names its mechanism. A draft that loads a motion library itself (GSAP, Lenis) is named per §3.6 item 18, and its calls are recorded through a sandbox-pinned copy of that library before recognition.

*Done when:* on Indus, the Heritage section's pinned horizontal track is recognised and mapped to `sgs/timeline` with the `pinned-horizontal` scroll effect; the brand pages' ingredient-card cursor reveal (`[data-peek]`: a circular `clip-path` image layer that follows the pointer), the `[data-mag]` magnet, the `[data-tilt]` tilt and the logo marquee are each recognised and either mapped or raised as a named Spec 38 gap; a planted effect with no SGS equivalent produces a gap row naming its mechanism; and each case fails with the recogniser removed.

Status: the effect recogniser is built (2026-10-22, Indus delivery U13 and U13b; the Heritage pinned track is U13a). `scripts/computed-route/lib/motion-behaviours.mjs::recogniseBehaviour` reads one animation record and recognises, by mechanism: a pointer handler writing a translate toward the pointer (magnet, normalised or distance pull; on a nav label the nav bar's `itemMagnetEnabled` and `itemMagnetStrength`), a slanted stripe sliding on pointer entry (sheen, with `skewX` as the angle), a circle growing on pointerdown (ripple), a pointer-written rotate (tilt: the hover state's follow-pointer with its maximum angle and spring back, plus a glare for the highlight it writes), pointer-written custom properties that a clip-path or mask circle consumes (the cursor field's `image-reveal`: rest and hover radius, radius duration and easing, ring; the file-drop opening is held by its full reveal), a scroll handler writing a translate by scrollY (parallax, signed ratio, page or view basis) or a scale of the scroll fraction (scroll progress), the marquee's playback-rate control (its `hoverRate`), the route fade (a site option) and the route scroll reset (native). `lib/fill-behaviour.mjs::applyBehaviours` writes them through `lib/motion-effects.mjs::writeEffect` (hover effects into `hover.effects`, the tilt into `hover.state`), a control after every node is written, and rings that the draft randomises per element store the values sampled on each element (`lib/motion-loops.mjs::loopEffect`, the float's drift; a moving scale is named as a gap). Intake raises one `effect` review row per recognised effect (`lib/intake-review.mjs`, marks `accept`, `block:<slug>`, `difference`). The clip reveals (U13b) are written through the existing clip channel with no corner radius (`lib/motion-clip.mjs::clipChannels` drops a `round` tail and names it). `scripts/computed-route/data/effect-settings.json` lists the block settings written directly. Proof: `tests/motion-behaviours.test.mjs`, `tests/motion-clip-reveal.test.mjs`, `tests/t2b-planted-draft.test.mjs`, each red under its mutations (`tests/fixtures/motion-behaviours/mutations.json`, `motion-clip-reveal`, `t2b-planted-draft`). Not built: the peek's full reveal on keyboard focus and tap (`cursor-field.js`, Track F), the Heritage track (U13a), the pointer-follow ring's own values.

### FR-48-78 — Motion writer (coverage wave 4.0)
**Read:** the re-derived animation inventory and timer-written styles (FR-48-47), the effects FR-48-77 recognises, and a draft's `marquee`. **Write:** per-element `sgsMotion` values through `scripts/computed-route/lib/motion-validate.mjs` (Spec 38A FR-38A-33, its phase M7), reading motion fields from the framework DB as Spec 38A decision 3 settles it, each value a token reference where it equals the client's token. **Check:** FR-48-47's parameter comparison and sampled pose. Existing requirements that own part of it: FR-48-25, FR-48-28, FR-48-47, FR-48-77.

*Done when:* `node scripts/computed-route/capabilities.mjs --gaps` lists no capability in the wave "motion writer (4.0)" (each is covered, with recognise, write and check references that resolve, or excluded by Bean) and `--gate` exits 0; a planted draft using one of the wave's capabilities raises no seen-but-unsupported row (FR-48-34) and its live page pairs with the draft at 375, 768 and 1440 for that capability; and with the writer removed the same draft raises the row again.

Status: built (2026-10-19, clone coverage step 4.0, `.claude/plans/archive/2026-10-12-clone-coverage-foundation.md`). The done-when holds: `node scripts/computed-route/capabilities.mjs --gaps` lists nothing in the wave "motion writer (4.0)" and `--gate` exits 0; `scripts/computed-route/tests/motion-planted-draft.test.mjs` plants a draft whose `<marquee>` (scrollamount 3, every 60ms, right: 50px/s) and SVG `<animate>` (opacity 1 to 0.4 and back over 2s) raise no seen-but-unsupported row through the real usage read and coverage map, raise each again with the writer's entry removed, and are written as validated layers; and on indus-test page 60 (`/motion-writer-proof/`, built from `sites/indus-foods/build/motion-proof.tree.json`), `sites/indus-foods/build/qa/probe-motion-proof.mjs` passes 27 of 27 at 375, 768 and 1440: the Indus draft's hero delays 80/190/300/410ms over 900ms, the scroll reveal at 850ms, grid items at 800ms with an 80ms step, the Indus entrance curve, the Indus marquee at 45px/s left (44.6 to 45.6 measured), and the planted pulse dipping to 0.40 every 1990 to 2022ms. A three-rater council (2026-10-19) reviewed the step; its findings are fixed (`17a84b42b`): only an endless animation is an effect (one the draft's own code starts only when the render shows it running untouched; a counted repeat or a visitor-started loop is reported), a move written after a flat turn or resize is rewritten exactly into the model's order and one no exact rewrite holds is reported, and the render check compares pose and easing as above. What it writes: entrances (multi-keyframe ones as `values.keyframes`, CSS `@keyframes` stops read by intake, clip-path insets as the clip channel), hovers (ring and accent-line shadows, perspective, resting tilts), and endless animations as `effects` layers (`lib/motion-loops.mjs`: marquee px/s, ambient float, SVG pulse, Ken Burns; `<marquee>` and SVG `<animate>`/`<animateTransform>`), every effect through `lib/motion-effects.mjs::writeEffect`, which FR-48-77's recognisers will call. A reverse run is written forwards with its keyframes mirrored and its easing mirrored (E'(x) = 1 - E(1 - x), `lib/motion-layers.mjs::asPlayed`; a session council found it written with the unmirrored curve, fixed 2026-10-19); a curve with no mirror is named. A loop no effect draws, or a value the motion model has no field for, is reported by name: skew, a slanted rotate3d, a true 3D matrix and `steps()` wait for Spec 38A FR-38A-57 (after the three test sites are cloned, Bean 2026-10-19). The pose sampled at 0, the midpoint and the end is compared too (FR-48-47, `lib/motion-sample.mjs`). Built 2026-10-18 (half A): `entrance.base` and `hover.state` per element (`scripts/computed-route/lib/fill-motion.mjs::motionWrites`, layers in `lib/motion-layers.mjs`, fields, targets and presets read from the framework DB by `lib/motion-db.mjs`, token references by `lib/motion-tokens.mjs`, every document through `lib/motion-validate.mjs`). Values come from the inventory's code; an entrance the draft's code picks at run time is matched to the render by its from-pose, a per-item delay the code computes is snapped to the code's own offset and step, and a record the render refutes at every width is not written. On the Indus home page and header (intake's proposals standing in for the skeleton) it writes 17 entrances, 9 hover moves, the brand strip's marquee and the run-time float. Bean ruled (2026-10-18, "Writer for all") that this writer writes every panel and effect, and that recognising an effect a draft builds in its own script is the behaviour recognisers wave (FR-48-77), so those coverage entries move to that wave with their write and check references set. An endless keyframe loop no effect draws (`lib/motion-loops.mjs::chooseEffect` reports it unmapped) with literal keyframes is written to the block root's Additional CSS box (`sgsCustomCss`, `sgsCustomCssId` `loop` plus the record id's letters and digits, `sgsCustomCssNote`; Spec 32 FR-32-20; a re-run adds nothing the box already holds) as `linear` timing over the fewest stops sampled from its whole-cycle easing that stay within 0.75deg and 0.005 scale (`lib/motion-loop-css.mjs`); a loop on a declared part is reported unmapped, since the box styles the block. `scripts/computed-route/tests/motion-loop-css.test.mjs` (8 tests) proves Mama's A-02 sparkle comes out as `tests/fixtures/motion-loop-css/A-02.expected.css`, which matched the draft live (Spec 32 FR-32-20, 2026-10-20).

### FR-48-79 — Modes and values with no CSS property

*Done when:* `node scripts/computed-route/capabilities.mjs --gaps` lists no capability in the wave "modes and values with no CSS property" (each is covered, with recognise, write and check references that resolve, or excluded by Bean) and `--gate` exits 0; a planted draft using one of the wave's capabilities raises no seen-but-unsupported row (FR-48-34) and its live page pairs with the draft at 375, 768 and 1440 for that capability; and with the writer removed the same draft raises the row again.

Status: not built. The setting meanings it needs are in the DB (Indus delivery U11, built by Track F; `F reseed-done eb57a037c`: `block_attributes.meaning` holds 50 meanings). It owns five of the CSS equivalence audit's unmatched 'setting' verdicts (2026-10-20, `.claude/plans/archive/2026-10-19-fr48-8-declared-values.md` Open): `overflow-x`, `scroll-snap-type`, `scroll-snap-align`, `grid-auto-columns` and `grid-auto-flow` are the sgs/container Scroll sideways mode (`scrollSideways`, a per-device on/off whose `css_property` `overflow` is outside calibration's read list and which has no marker, and `scrollItemWidth`, which paints only while it is on: `includes/container-scroll-row-css.php`); a draft's column-flowing grid maps to that mode by meaning, not by property. It also owns the sgs/hero `splitContentOrder` part of `order` (a per-device media-first or content-first mode with no `css_property` and no enum, painted as `order` only in the split variant: `hero/render.php`).

### FR-48-80 — Behaviour recognisers
**Read:** handlers, timers, observers, refs and data hooks with their targets. **Recognise** each behaviour by its mechanism (a timer advancing an index over an `sc-for` is a carousel with its autoplay interval, loop and dots; a handler toggling one panel of several is tabs or an accordion with its open state; a number written toward a target is a counter; a time difference written each second is a countdown; a class set past a scroll offset is a sticky header), and native `dialog`, `details` and popover. **Write:** the block and its behaviour settings, chosen by framework-DB query. **Check:** a scripted flow (§3.7) reaches the same states with the same timings. Existing requirements that own part of it: FR-48-34, FR-48-48, FR-48-77.

*Done when:* `node scripts/computed-route/capabilities.mjs --gaps` lists no capability in the wave "behaviour recognisers" (each is covered, with recognise, write and check references that resolve, or excluded by Bean) and `--gate` exits 0; a planted draft using one of the wave's capabilities raises no seen-but-unsupported row (FR-48-34) and its live page pairs with the draft at 375, 768 and 1440 for that capability; and with the recogniser removed the same draft raises the row again.

Status: partly built (2026-10-22, wave T2b, U14 and U13a, `7c5434cb1`, review `38af91ff6`, `e3614875b`): `scripts/computed-route/lib/motion-behaviours.mjs::recogniseBehaviour` recognises the script behaviours by mechanism, including from intake's formula-only records (`withEnds`): the scrolled header (`scrolledOffset`, `shadowScrolled`, `shadowScrolledColour`, `headerSticky`) and the top row hiding on scroll (`rowHideOnScroll`), the open-status dot pulse, scroll to the opened section, testimonial autoplay, the timeline's pinned panel settings, the drawer's exit, the route fade (a site option in Fill's `site-options.json`); `lib/fill-behaviour.mjs` writes them through a framework-DB attribute check, a behaviour on an element a block draws reaches that block (`lib/fill-motion.mjs::adoptDrawnBehaviours`), and every record on the surface ends as a write, an UNMAPPED row or a handover (`lib/fill-motion-account.mjs::silentRows`). The settings it writes have meanings in the DB (U11, `F reseed-done eb57a037c`). Not built: applying `site-options.json` at site build, the per-index stagger ladders, the drawer CTA bar rise (A-37), today's-row highlight (no record). Mama's behaviours join `.claude/plans/2026-10-22-mamas-delivery.md` when its units are written.
